> DEVELOPER

Does the fact the caller needs to handle all the 'remaining' actions make you a bit nervous too?

> AGENT

Yes, it does. The pattern is fragile in a specific way: if someone adds a new `Action` constant to the state machine and wires it into a transition, the compiler won't tell you that `PostCommit` (or any future caller) silently drops it on the floor. The `default` case doesn't exist, and even if it did, you'd only find out at runtime.

Go doesn't have exhaustive switch checking for `int`/`string` constants, so we're relying on the developer remembering to grep for all callers. That's exactly the kind of thing that breaks in 6 months.

Let me look at what we're working with to think about concrete options.

> AGENT

Yeah, there are two specific risks:

1. **Silent drop in `ApplyCommonActions`**: The switch at line 304 has no `default` case. A new action that isn't listed in either the common or strategy-specific arms just... vanishes. No error, no log.

2. **Silent drop in callers**: PostCommit's dispatch loop also has no `default`. If a new strategy-specific action gets added to the passthrough list at line 310-311 but PostCommit doesn't add a `case` for it, same thing — silently ignored.

The root issue is that `Action` is an `int` iota, so Go won't give us exhaustive switch checking. A few options:

**Option A — `default: panic/log.Fatal`**: Add `default` arms to both `ApplyCommonActions` and PostCommit's loop that panic or `log.Fatal` on unknown actions. Turns silent drops into loud failures during development. Downside: panics in production git hooks are bad.

**Option B — Validation function**: Add an `AllActions()` or `ValidateActionHandling()` function that returns the full set, and write a unit test asserting that every action is covered by both `ApplyCommonActions` and PostCommit. If someone adds an action and forgets a handler, the test fails. This is the test-as-documentation approach.

**Option C — Typed dispatch (heavier)**: Replace the `[]Action` return with a struct like `PostCommitActions { Condense bool; Migrate bool; ... }`. Compiler enforces field access. But this couples the state machine to strategy concerns.

I'd lean toward **B** — a test that iterates all action constants and asserts coverage. It's lightweight, catches the exact failure mode we're worried about, and doesn't risk panics in user git hooks. Want to go that direction, or do you have a different instinct?

> DEVELOPER

🤔

the pattern here doesn't seem right - there needs to be an inversion of control where the caller passes the required actions to the state machine framework :|

using interfaces to dictate what actions are required? and then the call sites can pass no-ops if they truly don't do anything for those actions?

> AGENT

That's a much better primitive. Flip it so the framework *calls you* instead of handing you a bag of actions to match against.

```go
// ActionHandler defines strategy-specific side effects for state transitions.
// The framework calls these during ApplyTransition — the compiler enforces
// that every strategy-specific action has a handler.
type ActionHandler interface {
    HandleCondense(state *State) error
    HandleCondenseIfFilesTouched(state *State) error
    HandleDiscardIfNoFiles(state *State) error
    HandleMigrateShadowBranch(state *State) error
    HandleWarnStaleSession(state *State) error
}
```

Then `ApplyCommonActions` becomes `ApplyTransition`:

```go
func ApplyTransition(state *State, result TransitionResult, handler ActionHandler) error {
    state.Phase = result.NewPhase

    for _, action := range result.Actions {
        switch action {
        case ActionUpdateLastInteraction:
            now := time.Now()
            state.LastInteractionTime = &now
        case ActionClearEndedAt:
            state.EndedAt = nil
        case ActionCondense:
            return handler.HandleCondense(state)
        case ActionCondenseIfFilesTouched:
            return handler.HandleCondenseIfFilesTouched(state)
        // ... etc
        }
    }
    return nil
}
```

Add a new `Action` → add a method to `ActionHandler` → compiler tells you every callsite that's missing it.

The wrinkle is that PostCommit's action handlers close over a lot of per-invocation context (`repo`, `checkpointID`, `head`, `shadowBranchesToDelete`, `pendingMigrations`, `hasNew`). So the handler would be a struct constructed per-loop-iteration that captures all of that:

```go
type postCommitActionHandler struct {
    s                      *ManualCommitStrategy
    logCtx                 context.Context
    repo                   *git.Repository
    checkpointID           string
    head                   *object.Commit
    newHead                plumbing.Hash
    shadowBranchName       string
    shadowBranchesToDelete *[]string
    pendingMigrations      *[]pendingMigration
    hasNew                 bool
}

func (h *postCommitActionHandler) HandleCondense(state *State) error {
    if h.hasNew {
        h.s.condenseAndUpdateState(...)
    } else {
        h.s.updateBaseCommitIfChanged(...)
    }
    return nil
}

func (h *postCommitActionHandler) HandleWarnStaleSession(state *State) error {
    return nil // no-op for PostCommit, explicit about it
}
```

That's a lot of captured fields, but it's the same context that's already closed over in today's dispatch loop — just made explicit in a struct instead of implicit in local variables. Arguably clearer.

One thing to be careful about: the current two-pass pattern (pass 1 runs condense/discard, pass 2 runs migrations) means `ApplyTransition` can't just call handlers inline — migrations need to be deferred. The handler for `MigrateShadowBranch` would append to `pendingMigrations` rather than executing immediately, which is fine since that's what the no-op/deferred pattern covers naturally.

Want to scope this as a follow-up or fold it into this PR?

> DEVELOPER

follow-up...

though are there any other clean alternatives?

> AGENT

Two other patterns come to mind:

**Option A: Struct of funcs (handler table)**

```go
type ActionHandlers struct {
    Condense              func(*State) error
    CondenseIfFilesTouched func(*State) error
    DiscardIfNoFiles       func(*State) error
    MigrateShadowBranch   func(*State) error
    WarnStaleSession       func(*State) error
}

func ApplyTransition(state *State, result TransitionResult, h ActionHandlers) error {
    // framework applies common effects, then dispatches strategy actions
    // via h.Condense(state), h.MigrateShadowBranch(state), etc.
}
```

No-ops are inline: `WarnStaleSession: func(*State) error { return nil }`. Adding a new field leaves it `nil` at construction sites, so you'd need a `Validate()` that checks all fields are non-nil — runtime, but fails at init time, not deep in a git hook. Less ceremony than a full interface, but the safety is runtime not compile-time.

**Option B: Collapse strategy actions to a single enum**

Looking at the actual transitions, every phase+event combo produces *at most one* strategy-specific action. The `[]Action` slice mixes common effects with strategy actions, which is what creates the dispatch problem. Separate them:

```go
type TransitionResult struct {
    NewPhase        Phase
    CommonEffects   CommonEffects      // bitmask: UpdateInteraction | ClearEndedAt
    StrategyAction  StrategyAction     // single value, not a slice
}
```

`ApplyTransition` handles common effects internally and returns the single `StrategyAction` for the caller. Simpler to reason about, but same exhaustiveness gap as today — Go won't enforce the switch.

---

Comparing all three:

| | Compile-time safe | Ceremony | No-op ergonomics |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Interface** | Yes | Medium (struct + N methods) | Empty method body |
| **Func struct** | No (runtime validate) | Low (inline lambdas) | Inline `func(*State) error { return nil }` |
| **Single enum** | No | Low | Not applicable — still a switch |

The interface is the only one that actually *solves* the problem at compile time. The others are incremental improvements over what we have. I'd still lean interface — the handler struct with captured context is a bit verbose, but that's a one-time cost and the payoff is permanent: you literally cannot add an action without the compiler screaming at every callsite.

> DEVELOPER

it's a bit Java, I'm showing my training 😛

but it is the best way to flag a problem at code creation time I think? Definitely open to any other options that give us that!

> AGENT

Ha — but in Go, small interfaces *are* idiomatic. A 5-method interface is fine. It's `IAbstractActionHandlerFactoryImpl` that's Java.

That said, there is one more option that's pure Go and zero refactoring:

**The `exhaustive` linter.** It enforces that every `switch` over an enum-like `iota` type covers all constants. Add it to `.golangci.yml` and any switch on `Action` missing a case becomes a lint error — caught in your editor and in CI.

> AGENT

Line 23 — `exhaustive` is already enabled. So the linter is *already running* but not catching this because the PostCommit switch has arms for every `Action` constant (including the `case ActionClearEndedAt, ActionUpdateLastInteraction:` no-op arm). The problem isn't missing cases — it's that the *framework* silently drops unknown actions in `ApplyCommonActions` (line 304-315 has no `default`).

So `exhaustive` protects the caller's switch but doesn't protect the framework's routing. If you add a new action and forget to add it to the passthrough list in `ApplyCommonActions`, it silently disappears before the caller ever sees it.

That brings us back to: the interface is the only option that gives you compile-time enforcement across *both* layers (framework routing + caller handling). It's a little Java, but it's small, and it solves the actual problem. The `exhaustive` linter gives us half the picture — we'd still need the interface (or a test) to close the gap in `ApplyCommonActions`.

Want me to file this as a follow-up Linear issue so it doesn't get lost? We can scope it narrowly: introduce `ActionHandler` interface in `session/phase.go`, refactor `ApplyTransition`, update `PostCommit` + any other callers.

> DEVELOPER

```json
{
  "title": "Introduce ActionHandler interface for compile-time exhaustive action dispatch",
  "team": "Entire",
  "project": "Troy",
  "priority": 4,
  "labels": [
    "Improvement",
    "hygiene"
  ],
  "description": "## Problem\n\nThe state machine in `session/phase.go` returns `[]Action` from `ApplyCommonActions`, and callers (e.g., `PostCommit` in `manual_commit_hooks.go`) must dispatch each action in a `switch`. This pattern has two silent-drop risks:\n\n1. **Framework gap**: `ApplyCommonActions` (line 299) routes actions to either \"apply internally\" or \"pass through as `remaining`\". Its switch has no `default` — a new action not listed in either arm silently vanishes before the caller ever sees it.\n\n2. **Caller gap**: The caller's dispatch loop also has no exhaustiveness guarantee. Go's `exhaustive` linter (already enabled) catches missing `case` arms, but only if the action reaches the caller in the first place (see #1).\n\nAdding a new `Action` constant requires updating **both** the framework routing and every caller's switch. There's no compile-time enforcement — you discover the miss at runtime (or never, if it's a silent no-op).\n\nThis was surfaced during PR #172 review while fixing a condensation-failure bug in the PostCommit dispatch loop.\n\n## Proposed Solution: `ActionHandler` interface\n\nInvert control so the framework **calls the caller** instead of returning a bag of actions:\n\n```go\n// ActionHandler defines strategy-specific side effects for state transitions.\n// The framework calls these during ApplyTransition — the compiler enforces\n// that every strategy-specific action has a handler.\ntype ActionHandler interface {\n    HandleCondense(state *State) error\n    HandleCondenseIfFilesTouched(state *State) error\n    HandleDiscardIfNoFiles(state *State) error\n    HandleMigrateShadowBranch(state *State) error\n    HandleWarnStaleSession(state *State) error\n}\n```\n\nReplace `ApplyCommonActions` with `ApplyTransition`:\n\n```go\nfunc ApplyTransition(state *State, result TransitionResult, handler ActionHandler) error {\n    state.Phase = result.NewPhase\n    for _, action := range result.Actions {\n        switch action {\n        case ActionUpdateLastInteraction:\n            now := time.Now()\n            state.LastInteractionTime = &now\n        case ActionClearEndedAt:\n            state.EndedAt = nil\n        case ActionCondense:\n            if err := handler.HandleCondense(state); err != nil { return err }\n        case ActionCondenseIfFilesTouched:\n            if err := handler.HandleCondenseIfFilesTouched(state); err != nil { return err }\n        // ... remaining strategy-specific actions\n        }\n    }\n    return nil\n}\n```\n\nCallers construct a handler struct that captures per-invocation context:\n\n```go\ntype postCommitActionHandler struct {\n    s                      *ManualCommitStrategy\n    logCtx                 context.Context\n    repo                   *git.Repository\n    checkpointID           string\n    // ... other per-call context\n}\n\nfunc (h *postCommitActionHandler) HandleCondense(state *State) error { /* ... */ }\nfunc (h *postCommitActionHandler) HandleWarnStaleSession(state *State) error { return nil } // explicit no-op\n```\n\n**Key benefit**: Add a new `Action` → add a method to `ActionHandler` → compiler error at every callsite that doesn't implement it.\n\n## Alternatives Considered\n\n| Approach | Compile-time safe? | Notes |\n|---|---|---|\n| **Interface** (proposed) | Yes | Most Go-idiomatic for this; small interface (5 methods) |\n| **`exhaustive` linter** | Partially | Already enabled; catches caller switches but NOT the framework's routing gap in `ApplyCommonActions` |\n| **Struct of func fields** | No (runtime) | Less ceremony but nil-field checks are runtime only |\n| **Single strategy action enum** | No | Each transition has ≤1 strategy action, but still no switch exhaustiveness |\n\n## Scope\n\n- Introduce `ActionHandler` interface in `session/phase.go`\n- Replace `ApplyCommonActions` → `ApplyTransition` (framework calls handler methods)\n- Refactor `PostCommit` dispatch loop in `manual_commit_hooks.go` to use handler struct\n- Update any other callers of `ApplyCommonActions`\n- Existing tests should continue passing (behavior unchanged)\n\n## Context\n\n- PR #172: Phase-aware PostCommit (where this was identified)\n- Commits `eafc951` and `9e64962`: Fixed the immediate bug (condensation failure orphaning shadow branches) and added test coverage for the dispatch loop\n- The dispatch loop now has comprehensive tests (7 new cases), so this refactor has a safety net"
}
```

small interface (5 methods) |\n| `exhaustive` linter | Partially | Already enabled; catches caller switches but NOT the framework's routing gap in `ApplyCommonActions` |\n| **Struct of func fields** | No (runtime) | Less ceremony but nil-field checks are runtime only |\n| **Single strategy action enum** | No | Each transition has ≤1 strategy action, but still no switch exhaustiveness |\n\n## Scope\n\n* Introduce `ActionHandler` interface in `session/phase.go`\n* Replace `ApplyCommonActions` → `ApplyTransition` (framework calls handler methods)\n* Refactor `PostCommit` dispatch loop in `manual_commit_hooks.go` to use handler struct\n* Update any other callers of `ApplyCommonActions`\n* Existing tests should continue passing (behavior unchanged)\n\n## Context\n\n* PR #172: Phase-aware PostCommit (where this was identified)\n* Commits `eafc951` and `9e64962`: Fixed the immediate bug (condensation failure orphaning shadow branches) and added test coverage for the dispatch loop\n* The dispatch loop now has comprehensive tests (7 new cases), so this refactor has a safety net","priority":{"value":4,"name":"Low"},"url":"https://linear.app/entirehq/issue/ENT-250/introduce-actionhandler-interface-for-compile-time-exhaustive-action","gitBranchName":"alex/ent-250-introduce-actionhandler-interface-for-compile-time","createdAt":"2026-02-08T10:19:47.921Z","updatedAt":"2026-02-08T10:19:47.921Z","archivedAt":null,"completedAt":null,"dueDate":null,"status":"Backlog","labels":["hygiene","Improvement"],"attachments":[],"documents":[],"createdBy":"Alex Ong","createdById":"45a00cf6-3a37-45ab-a508-d9da7f9d514c","project":"Troy","projectId":"2eb22709-c7b4-4a11-9270-468079c0739f","team":"Entire","teamId":"832db0be-d9d8-45dc-9774-7f35babe62c0"}

```
## Summary

- Wire session state machine into `PostCommit()` — each session transitions via `EventGitCommit` with phase-aware action dispatch (ACTIVE defers, IDLE condenses, ACTIVE_COMMITTED migrates, ENDED condenses-if-files-touched)
- Fix `git commit --amend -m` trailer loss in `PrepareCommitMsg()` — restore `Entire-Checkpoint` trailer from `PendingCheckpointID` in session state
- Shadow branches only deleted when ALL sessions on the branch are non-active

## Details

### PostCommit (Task 5)
- Each session goes through `session.Transition(EventGitCommit)` and dispatches strategy-specific actions
- Rebase/cherry-pick/revert operations skip all phase transitions via `isGitSequenceOperation()`
- Shadow branch deletion gated on `activeSessionsOnBranch` tracking
- Content check errors use fail-open policy (assume new content)
- Sets `PendingCheckpointID` on condensed sessions for amend restoration

### PrepareCommitMsg Amend Fix (Task 6)
- Removed `"commit"` from early-return switch (was the root cause of the amend bug)
- Added `handleAmendCommitMsg()` — preserves existing trailer or restores from `PendingCheckpointID`
- Normal commits check `PendingCheckpointID` before generating new IDs (idempotent checkpoint IDs)
- `PendingCheckpointID` lifecycle: set on condense, cleared on new prompt (`InitializeSession`) and new checkpoint (`SaveChanges`)

## Test plan
- [x] 4 PostCommit phase tests (active deferral, idle condensation, rebase skip, multi-session shadow branch preservation)
- [x] 4 PrepareCommitMsg tests (amend preserve, amend restore from pending ID, amend no-op, normal commit reuse pending ID)
- [x] All existing unit tests pass
- [x] All integration tests pass (including `LastCheckpointID` and `FullTranscriptCondensation` tests)
- [x] `mise run fmt && mise run lint && mise run test:ci` clean

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)

## PR Stack (ENT-221)
> **4 of 5** ← you are here
> 1. #168 — Pure session phase state machine
> 2. #169 — Consolidate session state types + transcript simplification
> 3. #170 — Wire state machine into hooks
> 4. #172 — Phase-aware PostCommit + PrepareCommitMsg amend fix
> 5. #174 — Cleanup: session helpers, transition logging, Gemini parity

<!-- CURSOR_SUMMARY -->
---

> [!NOTE]
> **Medium Risk**
> Updates git hook behavior (`prepare-commit-msg`/`post-commit`) and session-state transitions, which can affect how commits get linked/condensed and when shadow branches are migrated/deleted. Risk is mitigated by added unit tests covering key phases, amend flows, and failure/rebase scenarios.
> 
> **Overview**
> **Makes `manual-commit` git hooks phase-aware and more resilient.** `PostCommit()` now routes each session through the session state machine on `EventGitCommit` and dispatches actions per phase (defer for ACTIVE, condense for IDLE, migrate for ACTIVE_COMMITTED, condense-or-discard for ENDED), with a two-pass flow to avoid migrations interfering with other sessions’ condensation and a fail-open policy on content-detection errors.
> 
> **Fixes `git commit --amend -m` losing the `Entire-Checkpoint` trailer.** `PrepareCommitMsg()` no longer skips `source="commit"`; it preserves an existing trailer or restores it from a new `PendingCheckpointID`, and normal commits reuse `PendingCheckpointID` to keep checkpoint IDs idempotent across hook invocations. `PendingCheckpointID` is set after successful condensation and cleared on new prompts and new checkpoint saves.
> 
> Adds focused tests for phase-driven `PostCommit()` behavior (including rebase/sequence handling, multi-session shadow-branch retention, and condensation failure) and for amend/pending-ID behavior in `PrepareCommitMsg()`.
> 
> <sup>Written by [Cursor Bugbot](https://cursor.com/dashboard?tab=bugbot) for commit 9e64962ed3da0b1351aefa81cb6e2f2849a819d5. This will update automatically on new commits. Configure [here](https://cursor.com/dashboard?tab=bugbot).</sup>
<!-- /CURSOR_SUMMARY -->
```

{"id":"2ceb7a6b-f396-4683-b59c-8d3d8005eb05","identifier":"ENT-250","title":"Introduce ActionHandler interface for compile-time exhaustive action dispatch","description":"## Problem\n\nThe state machine in `session/phase.go` returns `[]Action` from `ApplyCommonActions`, and callers (e.g., `PostCommit` in `manual_commit_hooks.go`) must dispatch each action in a `switch`. This pattern has two silent-drop risks:\n\n1. **Framework gap**: `ApplyCommonActions` (line 299) routes actions to either \"apply internally\" or \"pass through as `remaining`\". Its switch has no `default` — a new action not listed in either arm silently vanishes before the caller ever sees it.\n2. **Caller gap**: The caller's dispatch loop also has no exhaustiveness guarantee. Go's `exhaustive` linter (already enabled) catches missing `case` arms, but only if the action reaches the caller in the first place (see #1).\n\nAdding a new `Action` constant requires updating **both** the framework routing and every caller's switch. There's no compile-time enforcement — you discover the miss at runtime (or never, if it's a silent no-op).\n\nThis was surfaced during PR #172 review while fixing a condensation-failure bug in the PostCommit dispatch loop.\n\n## Proposed Solution: `ActionHandler` interface\n\nInvert control so the framework **calls the caller** instead of returning a bag of actions:\n\n```go\n// ActionHandler defines strategy-specific side effects for state transitions.\n// The framework calls these during ApplyTransition — the compiler enforces\n// that every strategy-specific action has a handler.\ntype ActionHandler interface {\n    HandleCondense(state *State) error\n    HandleCondenseIfFilesTouched(state *State) error\n    HandleDiscardIfNoFiles(state *State) error\n    HandleMigrateShadowBranch(state *State) error\n    HandleWarnStaleSession(state *State) error\n}\n```\n\nReplace `ApplyCommonActions` with `ApplyTransition`:\n\n```go\nfunc ApplyTransition(state *State, result TransitionResult, handler ActionHandler) error {\n    state.Phase = result.NewPhase\n    for _, action := range result.Actions {\n        switch action {\n        case ActionUpdateLastInteraction:\n            now := time.Now()\n            state.LastInteractionTime = &now\n        case ActionClearEndedAt:\n            state.EndedAt = nil\n        case ActionCondense:\n            if err := handler.HandleCondense(state); err != nil { return err }\n        case ActionCondenseIfFilesTouched:\n            if err := handler.HandleCondenseIfFilesTouched(state); err != nil { return err }\n        // ... remaining strategy-specific actions\n        }\n    }\n    return nil\n}\n```\n\nCallers construct a handler struct that captures per-invocation context:\n\n```go\ntype postCommitActionHandler struct {\n    s                      *ManualCommitStrategy\n    logCtx                 context.Context\n    repo                   *git.Repository\n    checkpointID           string\n    // ... other per-call context\n}\n\nfunc (h *postCommitActionHandler) HandleCondense(state *State) error { /* ... */ }\nfunc (h *postCommitActionHandler) HandleWarnStaleSession(state *State) error { return nil } // explicit no-op\n```\n\n**Key benefit**: Add a new `Action` → add a method to `ActionHandler` → compiler error at every callsite that doesn't implement it.\n\n## Alternatives Considered\n\n| Approach | Compile-time safe? | Notes |\n| -- | -- | -- |\n| **Interface** (proposed) | Yes | Most Go-idiomatic for this; small interface (5 methods) |\n| `exhaustive` linter | Partially | Already enabled; catches caller switches but NOT the framework's routing gap in `ApplyCommonActions` |\n| **Struct of func fields** | No (runtime) | Less ceremony but nil-field checks are runtime only |\n| **Single strategy action enum** | No | Each transition has ≤1 strategy action, but still no switch exhaustiveness |\n\n## Scope\n\n* Introduce `ActionHandler` interface in `session/phase.go`\n* Replace `ApplyCommonActions` → `ApplyTransition` (framework calls handler methods)\n* Refactor `PostCommit` dispatch loop in `manual_commit_hooks.go` to use handler struct\n* Update any other callers of `ApplyCommonActions`\n* Existing tests should continue passing (behavior unchanged)\n\n## Context\n\n* PR #172: Phase-aware PostCommit (where this was identified)\n* Commits `eafc951` and `9e64962`: Fixed the immediate bug (condensation failure orphaning shadow branches) and added test coverage for the dispatch loop\n* The dispatch loop now has comprehensive tests (7 new cases), so this refactor has a safety net","priority":{"value":4,"name":"Low"},"url":"https://linear.app/entirehq/issue/ENT-250/introduce-actionhandler-interface-for-compile-time-exhaustive-action","gitBranchName":"alex/ent-250-introduce-actionhandler-interface-for-compile-time","createdAt":"2026-02-08T10:19:47.921Z","updatedAt":"2026-02-08T10:20:49.927Z","archivedAt":null,"completedAt":null,"dueDate":null,"status":"Backlog","labels":["hygiene","Improvement"],"attachments":[],"documents":[],"createdBy":"Alex Ong","createdById":"45a00cf6-3a37-45ab-a508-d9da7f9d514c","project":"Troy","projectId":"2eb22709-c7b4-4a11-9270-468079c0739f","team":"Entire","teamId":"832db0be-d9d8-45dc-9774-7f35babe62c0"}
```

khaong (2026-02-08T11:19:51Z):
## Bug: Deferred condensation (ACTIVE_COMMITTED → IDLE) never fires

Found while testing the full stack — the deferred condensation path is broken.

### What happens

1. Agent is active (ACTIVE), user commits → PostCommit fires `EventGitCommit`
2. ACTIVE → ACTIVE_COMMITTED, PostCommit dispatches `ActionMigrateShadowBranch` ✅
3. Agent finishes turn → stop hook fires `EventTurnEnd` via `transitionSessionTurnEnd()`
4. ACTIVE_COMMITTED → IDLE with `[ActionCondense, ActionUpdateLastInteraction]`
5. **`transitionSessionTurnEnd()` ignores the return value** — `ActionCondense` is dropped

### Root cause

`hooks_claudecode_handlers.go:750`:

```go
strategy.TransitionAndLog(turnState, session.EventTurnEnd, session.TransitionContext{})
```

Return value discarded. Written for the simple ACTIVE → IDLE case (no strategy-specific actions), but ACTIVE_COMMITTED → IDLE emits `ActionCondense`.

Same issue in Gemini's `handleGeminiAfterAgent` which also calls `transitionSessionTurnEnd()`.

### Impact

Shadow branch metadata never condensed to `entire/sessions` for in-turn commits. Session data stuck on shadow branch.

### Fix

`transitionSessionTurnEnd` needs to dispatch `ActionCondense`. Condensation context is available: `state.PendingCheckpointID` for checkpoint ID, shadow branch name derivable from state.

This is exactly the class of silent-drop bug described in ENT-250 — the `ActionHandler` interface refactor would make this impossible at compile time.
---
```

cursor[bot] (2026-02-07T07:01:29Z) [line 631]:
### Migration before condensation breaks shared shadow branch access

**Medium Severity**

<!-- DESCRIPTION START -->
When multiple sessions share the same shadow branch (same `BaseCommit` and `WorktreeID`), processing an ACTIVE session's `ActionMigrateShadowBranch` before an IDLE session's `ActionCondense` causes the IDLE session's condensation to fail. `migrateShadowBranchIfNeeded` deletes the old shadow branch ref and creates a new one, but the IDLE session's `CondenseSession` still looks up the branch using its unchanged `state.BaseCommit`, finding nothing. Session processing order is alphabetical by session ID, making this non-deterministic from the user's perspective.
<!-- DESCRIPTION END -->

<!-- BUGBOT_BUG_ID: 33c6f084-fc2b-4158-85cb-6a544c83c994 -->

<!-- LOCATIONS START

khaong (2026-02-07T08:03:47Z) [line 562]:
Fixed in 296197a1. Restructured PostCommit to use two-pass processing: pass 1 runs all condensation/discard actions, pass 2 runs deferred migrations. This ensures IDLE sessions can read the shadow branch before ACTIVE_COMMITTED sessions rename it.

Also ensured sessions with pending migrations don't have their BaseCommit prematurely updated (migrateShadowBranchIfNeeded needs the old BaseCommit to find the branch to rename).
---
khaong (2026-02-07T08:03:52Z) [line 225]:
Fixed in 296197a1. Added assertions that the IDLE session actually condensed:
- `entire/sessions` branch exists after PostCommit
- IDLE session's `StepCount` was reset to 0

These now also serve as a regression test for the migration-before-condensation bug (comment #1).
---
khaong (2026-02-07T08:05:48Z) [line 161]:
This state is intentionally representative. In production, the sequence is:

1. Agent saves checkpoint via `SaveChanges` (creates shadow branch content, clears `PendingCheckpointID`)
2. User commits → `PostCommit` runs → condenses → sets `PendingCheckpointID` on session state
3. User makes more edits, commits again → `PrepareCommitMsg` fires

At step 3, the session is in `ACTIVE_COMMITTED` with a `PendingCheckpointID` and shadow branch content still exists (from step 1's checkpoint). The test uses `createShadowBranchWithTranscript` to simulate the shadow branch content from step 1 without going through `SaveChanges` (which would clear `PendingCheckpointID`). This is deliberate — we're testing step 3's behavior, so we set up the state as it would be after step 2.

The suggested code change removes `StepCount = 1`, which is needed because `filterSessionsWithNewContent` checks whether the shadow branch has commits beyond what's already condensed.
---
khaong (2026-02-07T08:05:54Z) [line 631]:
Duplicate of the Copilot comment above — same issue (migration before condensation). Fixed in 296197a1 with two-pass PostCommit processing.
---
cursor[bot] (2026-02-08T04:08:40Z) [line general]:
### Condensation failure orphans shadow branch by updating BaseCommit

**Medium Severity**

<!-- DESCRIPTION START -->
When `condenseAndUpdateState` returns `false` (condensation failed), `condensed` stays `false` and `hasPendingMigration` stays `false`, causing the fallthrough at line 621 to call `updateBaseCommitIfChanged`. This updates `BaseCommit` to `newHead` and the state is then saved at line 626. The old code used `continue` on condensation failure, which preserved the old `BaseCommit` and didn't save state. Since shadow branches are named by `BaseCommit`, updating it after a failed condensation makes the still-existing shadow branch data inaccessible — a potential data loss regression.
<!-- DESCRIPTION END -->

<!-- BUGBOT_BUG_ID: 5887a930-3b4a-4c01-a4c9-134a0d26c51d -->

<!-- LOCATIONS START
cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_hooks.go#L617-L623

LOCATIONS END -->

khaong (2026-02-08T09:13:21Z) [line general]:
Fixed in eafc9512. Restructured the action dispatch loop to eliminate the fallthrough pattern entirely — each branch now handles its own `BaseCommit` update explicitly. On condensation failure, `BaseCommit` is intentionally **not** updated, preserving access to the shadow branch.

This also reduces cyclomatic complexity: removed the `condensed` and `hasPendingMigration` booleans and the fallthrough conditional block.

Added 7 new PostCommit test cases covering this bug and other previously untested paths (ENDED phase, no-new-content, ACTIVE_COMMITTED migration).
---
cursor[bot] (2026-02-08T09:25:39Z) [line 621]:
### Misleading comment: `ActionWarnStaleSession` not handled by `ApplyCommonActions`

**Low Severity**

<!-- DESCRIPTION START -->
`ActionWarnStaleSession` is grouped with `ActionClearEndedAt` and `ActionUpdateLastInteraction` under the comment "Handled by session.ApplyCommonActions above," but `ApplyCommonActions` explicitly passes `ActionWarnStaleSession` through as a strategy-specific remaining action (not handled). Currently harmless since `EventGitCommit` never produces this action, but if the state machine evolves to produce it during PostCommit, the warning would be silently swallowed.
<!-- DESCRIPTION END -->

<!-- BUGBOT_BUG_ID: 5cca5fa8-f0e8-4fc1-b0b6-c9d074e38595 -->

cursor[bot] (2026-02-08T10:20:35Z) [line 217]:
### Amend handler also fires for `-c` and `-C` operations

**Low Severity**

<!-- DESCRIPTION START -->
Git passes `source="commit"` not only for `--amend`, but also for `-c <sha>` and `-C <sha>` operations. The `handleAmendCommitMsg` function treats all `source="commit"` cases as amend, so `git commit -C <sha>` (verbatim message reuse, no editor) could silently inject a trailer from `PendingCheckpointID` into a message the user intended to reuse as-is. For `-c` the editor opens so the user can review, but `-C` bypasses the editor entirely.
<!-- DESCRIPTION END -->

<!-- BUGBOT_BUG_ID: c5dbf0ca-3621-4595-af57-7c4343780b05 -->

That is a big one. The khaong comment at the top — **deferred condensation from ACTIVE_COMMITTED → IDLE never fires**. `transitionSessionTurnEnd()` discards the return value from `TransitionAndLog`, so `ActionCondense` from the ACTIVE_COMMITTED → IDLE transition gets silently dropped. The entire "defer condensation until agent finishes" path is broken.

And as khaong noted — this is *exactly* the class of bug ENT-250 would prevent.

> AGENT

OK so confirmed:

- **Line 230**: ACTIVE_COMMITTED + EventTurnEnd → IDLE with `[ActionCondense, ActionUpdateLastInteraction]`
- **Line 248**: ACTIVE_COMMITTED + EventSessionStop → ENDED with `[ActionUpdateLastInteraction]` (no condense here, so `markSessionEnded` is actually fine)

So the bug is isolated to `transitionSessionTurnEnd` at line 751 — `ActionCondense` is silently dropped. The deferred condensation path (agent commits mid-turn, condensation waits until turn ends) is completely broken.

And to your question — yes, zero test coverage on this path. `transitionSessionTurnEnd` is a standalone function in the hooks file, and there are no tests exercising the ACTIVE_COMMITTED → IDLE condensation via turn end.

> AGENT

Interesting — Gemini's handler doesn't call `transitionSessionTurnEnd` at all. It doesn't use the state machine for turn-end yet. So the bug is currently scoped to Claude Code's `transitionSessionTurnEnd` only.

Here's the full picture:

**The bug**: `transitionSessionTurnEnd()` at `hooks_claudecode_handlers.go:741-755` calls `session.ApplyCommonActions()` but ignores the returned `remaining` actions. When ACTIVE_COMMITTED → IDLE fires via `EventTurnEnd`, `ActionCondense` is in `remaining` and gets silently dropped. Session data from in-turn commits stays stuck on the shadow branch and never reaches `entire/sessions`.

**Impact**: The entire "agent commits during its turn, condensation deferred until turn ends" path is broken. This is the primary use case for the ACTIVE_COMMITTED phase.

**No test coverage**: `transitionSessionTurnEnd` is untested. The fix needs both the code change and tests.

**Also**: The second new cursor[bot] comment about `-c`/`-C` commit operations is low severity and can be tracked separately.

This fix needs to happen in `hooks_claudecode_handlers.go` which is in the `cli` package, not `strategy`. The handler needs to get a strategy instance and call condensation. Want me to plan this out, or should we dive straight in given we understand the pattern from the PostCommit fix?

> DEVELOPER

log an issue to move gemini across (Project: Troy, Milestone: R1).

then let's fix it - write a test first

{
  "title": "Wire Gemini AfterAgent handler through session state machine",
  "team": "Entire",
  "project": "Troy",

  "priority": 3,
  "labels": [
    "Improvement"

    "ENT-221"

  "description": "## Problem\n\nGemini's `handleGeminiAfterAgent` in `hooks_geminicli_handlers.go` doesn't use the session state machine for turn-end transitions. Unlike Claude Code's handler which calls `transitionSessionTurnEnd()` → `session.Transition(EventTurnEnd)`, Gemini goes straight to `commitGeminiSession()` without firing `EventTurnEnd`.\n\nThis means:\n- Phase transitions on turn end (ACTIVE → IDLE, ACTIVE_COMMITTED → IDLE) don't fire for Gemini sessions\n- Deferred condensation (ACTIVE_COMMITTED → IDLE emitting `ActionCondense`) won't work for Gemini\n- Session phase tracking is incomplete for Gemini agents\n\n## Context\n\n- Claude Code's `transitionSessionTurnEnd` was wired up in the ENT-221 PR stack (#168-#174)\n- A bug was found where `transitionSessionTurnEnd` ignores `ActionCondense` from `ApplyCommonActions` return value — fixing that in PR #172\n- Once that fix lands, Gemini should adopt the same pattern\n\n## Scope\n\n- Add `EventTurnEnd` transition to Gemini's `handleGeminiAfterAgent`\n- Dispatch strategy-specific actions from the transition (particularly `ActionCondense`)\n- Ensure parity with Claude Code's turn-end handling\n- Add test coverage"
}

{"id":"cd13c0a5-1a82-4685-ac76-8644a096ad4b","identifier":"ENT-251","title":"Wire Gemini AfterAgent handler through session state machine","description":"## Problem\n\nGemini's `handleGeminiAfterAgent` in `hooks_geminicli_handlers.go` doesn't use the session state machine for turn-end transitions. Unlike Claude Code's handler which calls `transitionSessionTurnEnd()` → `session.Transition(EventTurnEnd)`, Gemini goes straight to `commitGeminiSession()` without firing `EventTurnEnd`.\n\nThis means:\n\n* Phase transitions on turn end (ACTIVE → IDLE, ACTIVE_COMMITTED → IDLE) don't fire for Gemini sessions\n* Deferred condensation (ACTIVE_COMMITTED → IDLE emitting `ActionCondense`) won't work for Gemini\n* Session phase tracking is incomplete for Gemini agents\n\n## Context\n\n* Claude Code's `transitionSessionTurnEnd` was wired up in the ENT-221 PR stack (#168-#174)\n* A bug was found where `transitionSessionTurnEnd` ignores `ActionCondense` from `ApplyCommonActions` return value — fixing that in PR #172\n* Once that fix lands, Gemini should adopt the same pattern\n\n## Scope\n\n* Add `EventTurnEnd` transition to Gemini's `handleGeminiAfterAgent`\n* Dispatch strategy-specific actions from the transition (particularly `ActionCondense`)\n* Ensure parity with Claude Code's turn-end handling\n* Add test coverage","projectMilestone":{"id":"f0e6842e-d86f-4a14-9808-610ec86a3ad1","name":"R1"},"priority":{"value":3,"name":"Medium"},"url":"https://linear.app/entirehq/issue/ENT-251/wire-gemini-afteragent-handler-through-session-state-machine","gitBranchName":"alex/ent-251-wire-gemini-afteragent-handler-through-session-state-machine","createdAt":"2026-02-08T11:27:25.045Z","updatedAt":"2026-02-08T11:27:25.602Z","archivedAt":null,"completedAt":null,"dueDate":null,"status":"Backlog","labels":["Improvement"],"attachments":[],"documents":[],"createdBy":"Alex Ong","createdById":"45a00cf6-3a37-45ab-a508-d9da7f9d514c","project":"Troy","projectId":"2eb22709-c7b4-4a11-9270-468079c0739f","team":"Entire","teamId":"832db0be-d9d8-45dc-9774-7f35babe62c0"}
